# SMH Priced-Calm Conditioning v0: VXN at the entry (the last covariate), and the stability audit of the actuarial table

Status: DESIGN BOOKKEEPING on the UNSEALED pre-2021 sample only (rule-set-V class). Date filter date < 2021-01-01 throughout; holdout 2021+ SPENT and never touched. Built 12 Nov 2026, session 43, PROGRESS block 51, on Andrea's "continue developing the market theoretical framework". Nothing here is an inferential claim, a test, or a trade; the forward protocol is unchanged. All constants below are recomputed and asserted by the smh_theorem.py selftest (verify_all's smh_theorem check; the bundle stays at 20 checks).

Two questions this layer answers. First: the unseen-ness layer (docs/smh_unseen_v0.md) closed the information door at the level and the mean - but it left one covariate question standing: does the PRICE of fear at the cross carry hazard information beyond the region itself? Second: the cycle layer's actuarial table and proof timeline rest on the design trigger rate - is that rate stable within the sample?

## 1. The split (VXN at the compression entry, median cut)

The 41 pre-2021 compression entries carry full VXN coverage (session 40). Split at the entry median VXN = 16.47 (the entries' own median, itself far below the all-weeks 20.14 - the level-cheap finding):

| half | n | events | person-weeks | 12-week cum | Greenwood 95% |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| deep-calm (VXN <= 16.47) | 21 | 10 | 184 | 0.476 | [0.263, 0.690] |
| residual-fear (VXN > 16.47) | 20 | 16 | 143 | 0.800 | [0.625, 0.975] |

Person-week tiling: 184 + 143 = 327 = the frozen total; no mass lost in the split.

The quartile cut (percentile of VXN within the pre-2021 weekly distribution) shows the same shape: bottom-quartile entries (pct <= 0.25, n = 20): 9 events, cum 0.450 [0.232, 0.668]; middle-band entries (n = 20): 16 events, 0.800 [0.625, 0.975]; the top quartile contains a single entry (n = 1, no content).

THE DIRECTION OF THE SPLIT: deeper priced calm at the cross associates with LOWER hazard; the crosses that convert most often are the ones where fear is still residually priced. THE STRONGEST COMPRESSION CROSSES CARRY RESIDUAL FEAR, NOT MAXIMUM COMPLACENCY.

## 2. Why this is a flagged seductive cell, not a finding

1. BANDS OVERLAP. [0.263, 0.690] versus [0.625, 0.975]: no separation. The selftest carries an honesty guard asserting the overlap persists - if the bands ever separated on recomputation, the framing of this section would have to be revisited, and the guard fails the build.
2. THE ERA CONFOUND IS SEVERE. The deep-calm half is concentrated in 2005-07 and 2013-18 (the complacent eras); the residual-fear half in 2004-13 and 2019 (crisis aftermath). With n = 21/20 spread across two decades, "VXN carries information" cannot be separated from "the crisis-aftermath era produced more events per trigger".
3. A NEARBY MECHANICAL CUT DOES NOT RESOLVE IT. Entries within 52 weeks of a stress week: 35 entries, 23 events, cum 0.657; beyond: 6 entries, 3 events, 0.500 - the recency cut points the same way but its far cell holds only 6 entries. The halves' median weeks-since-last-stress-week are 11.5 (residual-fear) versus 21.0 (deep-calm): the split is partly a recency proxy. Origin-region does not explain it (both halves entered from mid in 19 and 18 of 21 cases).
4. THE DISCIPLINE, RESTATED: this is the largest seductive cell the design has produced (a 0.32 cum gap on a 20/21 split). It is recorded, flagged, and never trusted until forward-scored. Nothing in the forward protocol, the probabilities, or the rows changes because of it.

The theoretical reading IF it ever survives forward scoring: not all boredom is equal - the boredom that follows drama would be the dangerous boredom, the boredom of long complacency the weak one. The doctrine gains a candidate refinement, nothing more: WATCH THE CROSS THAT COMES WITH FEAR STILL PRICED. The echo of the stress clock, not the depth of the calm, may be the strong signature.

## 3. The stability audit of the actuarial table

The cycle layer froze 2.04 triggers per year and hung the proof timeline on it. Audited within the design sample (1050 valid weeks, ends truncated - the 2000-2004 bucket starts late in 2000, the 2020 bucket stops at the design end):

| 5-year bucket | triggers | significant weeks |
|---|---|---|
| 2000-2004 | 3 | 7 |
| 2005-2009 | 12 | 11 |
| 2010-2014 | 16 | 15 |
| 2015-2019 | 10 | 11 |
| 2020 | 0 | 3 |

1. THE TRIGGER RATE IS REGIME-DEPENDENT: 0.7 to 3.2 per year across the buckets, a 4.5x spread within one 20-year sample. The actuarial table's triggers-per-year is an average over regimes, not a constant of nature. The kill-gate YEARS column of the cycle doc inherits this: those years are arithmetic on the average rate, and the average is the weakest number in the framework.
2. THE CAPTURE FRACTION IS THE STABLE LEG. Split the sample in half: first half (2000-09-22 to 2010-11-05): 17 triggers, 11 events = 0.647; second half (2010-11-12 to 2020-12-25): 24 triggers, 15 events = 0.625; overall 0.634. The event-per-trigger probability - the actual object of the kill lines - moves by 0.022 across halves while the trigger rate moves by a factor of 4.5. ROWS ARE STABLE, YEARS ARE NOT.
3. THE DESIGN VERDICT ON THE PROOF TIMELINE, SHARPENED: the kill lines were pre-declared row-counted, never clock-counted, and this audit is the measured justification. The gates open when the rows exist; the years column is illustrative and now known to be the soft part of the illustration. If the live trigger regime runs hot (2010-14 style), the first gate could arrive in ~3 years; if it runs cold (2000-04 style), nearer 15.

## 4. Caveats, frozen

1. Both splits: n = 21/20 and 35/6; episode dependence everywhere; no test is run and none is implied.
2. The median 16.47 is the entries' own median - an in-sample cutpoint chosen after seeing the data, one more reason the cell stays flagged.
3. The stability buckets are truncated at both ends and the years are calendar buckets, not regime definitions; the 4.5x spread is descriptive, not a regime model.
4. No rule, probability, kill line, or protocol value changed; T, V, V4h untouched; nothing booked; the forward log stays empty by design.

## 5. Honest computation log

- The first scratch pass at the split crashed with an unguarded KM division on the top-quartile cell (n = 1: at-risk mass runs out at some h, division by zero); caught immediately, guarded in the shipped computation, and the single-entry cell is recorded as no-content rather than excluded silently.
- A missing import (HIGH) aborted one exploratory run before any number was read; rerun clean.
- The constants were first frozen with 2-tuple bands (lo, hi) against a computation returning 3-tuples (lo, point, hi); the selftest caught the shape mismatch on its first run (index error, not a silent drift). All band constants are now the 3-tuple (lo, point, hi) shape, asserted field by field.

# SMH Proof Criteria v0: every claim's pass/fail schedule, pre-declared with measured operating characteristics

Status: DESIGN BOOKKEEPING on the UNSEALED pre-2021 sample only (rule-set-V class). Date filter date < 2021-01-01 throughout; holdout 2021+ SPENT and never touched. Built 12 Nov 2026, session 44, PROGRESS block 52, on Andrea's "continue developing the hypothesis and proof of criteria"; extended the same day, session 46, PROGRESS block 54, with section 8 (the readout operating characteristics). Nothing here is an inferential claim, a test, or a trade; the forward protocol is unchanged. All constants below are recomputed and asserted by the smh_theorem.py selftest (verify_all's smh_theorem check; the bundle stays at 20 checks). THE FORWARD LOG IS EMPTY: every schedule in this doc is pre-declared before a single forward row exists. That ordering is the whole point of the layer.

What this layer adds: the theorem card carries measured design numbers; the kill-line doc carried bare thresholds. This doc completes the falsification machinery by measuring the GATES THEMSELVES: what a gate costs a true machine (false-kill), what it does to a false one (power), what each scoring readout should show under each hypothesis (calibration expectations), when direction may be re-examined (two pre-declared peeks), and which conditional cells are ever allowed to be scored (a closed register). Proof is no longer "we will see"; it is a schedule.

## 1. The occurrence gates and their operating characteristics

The kill-line convention (smh_theorem.py kill_line): the largest k with P(Bin(n, p0) <= k) <= 0.05, i.e. the 5% quantile of the binomial under the design's own p0.

Compression occurrence claim (p0 = 0.634, 26/41 design), thresholds frozen 3/8/19/31 at n = 10/20/40/60 resolved forward rows:

| n rows | kill if events <= | false-kill (true 0.634) | power (climatology 0.441) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10 | 3 | 0.0335 | 0.2855 |
| 20 | 8 | 0.0282 | 0.4465 |
| 40 | 19 | 0.0290 | 0.7243 |
| 60 | 31 | 0.0415 | 0.9046 |

Reading: each individual gate kills a TRUE machine about 3-4% of the time (that is the falsificationist's fee), while a climatology world (the measured 0.441, the do-nothing alternative) dies at the gates with probability 29/45/72/90% as rows accumulate. By n = 60 the schedule is well-powered against the only measured rival. Against a coin (0.5) the gates are weaker (power 0.179/0.265/0.457/0.668) because 0.5 sits closer to 0.634 than 0.441 does; the coin is not the measured alternative and is not the design's rival.

Stress de-stress claim (p0 = 0.636, 21/33 design): the kill lines are 3/8/19/31, the SAME thresholds as the compression gates (0.636 and 0.634 round to the same 5% binomial quantile at these n). False-kill 0.0325/0.0270/0.0272/0.0386; power against a coin 0.1719/0.2517/0.4373/0.6506. The stress gates are honestly weaker: their design edge over a coin is smaller than the compression edge over climatology, so their schedule discriminates more slowly. Recorded as is; no asymmetric tuning applied.

THE CUMULATIVE HONESTY STATEMENT: under an independence approximation, the probability a TRUE 0.634 machine dies somewhere in the four-gate schedule is ~0.126 (1 minus the product of the survivals). The approximation overstates independence (the gates share rows, so the true cumulative is lower), but the point stands and is stated in advance: A TRUE MACHINE CAN DIE IN PUBLIC, ROUGHLY ONE RUN IN EIGHT, AND THAT IS THE PRICE OF PUBLIC FALSIFIABILITY. The alternative - gates so loose no true machine ever dies - is unfalsifiable decoration and was refused.

## 2. The calibration expectations (what the reliability readouts compare against)

The ftep protocol scores forward rows with natural-log logloss and Brier, with reliability readouts at 52 and 104 scored windows and matched climatology rows scored alongside. Expected values per resolved 12-week window, computed from the frozen design numbers only:

If the design is right (outcomes Bernoulli 0.634 at compression entries):

| forecast | expected logloss (nats) | expected Brier |
|---|---|---|
| SMH 0.634 | 0.6568 | 0.2320 |
| climatology 0.441 | 0.7319 | 0.2693 |

If climatology is right (outcomes Bernoulli 0.441 everywhere):

| forecast | expected logloss (nats) | expected Brier |
|---|---|---|
| SMH 0.634 | 0.7628 | 0.2838 |
| climatology 0.441 | 0.6862 | 0.2465 |

The expected edge if the design is right is 0.0751 nats/row; the expected loss if wrong is 0.0766 nats/row. Symmetric magnitudes by construction (cross-entropy arithmetic), which is exactly what makes the readout a fair trial: the scoring rule cannot favour the design's hypothesis on expectation unless the outcomes actually favour it. THE FORWARD READOUTS AT 52/104 WINDOWS ARE JUDGED AGAINST THESE FOUR NUMBERS, declared before the first window.

## 3. The direction gates (two pre-declared peeks)

Direction was refused at design (17/26 up, CP95 [0.443, 0.828]; lower bound below 0.5; the selftest asserts the refusal). It is re-examined ONLY at two pre-declared points, counted in resolved forward first EVENTS, not rows:

| peek | promotion (all-up) | down-bias annotation |
|---|---|---|
| n = 40 events | up >= 27/40 (CP95 lower > 0.5) | up <= 13/40 (CP95 upper < 0.5) |
| n = 60 events | up >= 39/60 | up <= 21/60 |

Promotion means and only means: the doctrine's direction sentence may be re-armed as a hypothesis with a measured band. Down-bias triggers an annotation, never a trade change. Timing honesty: 40 events is ~63 rows at the design capture rate, roughly 31 years at the design trigger rate, arriving even later than the n = 60 occurrence gate. Direction is the slowest claim in the framework and is treated as such. Two peeks, both declared now; no other looks.

## 4. The seductive-cell register (CLOSED)

Three conditional cells, each frozen at its design definition, are eligible for forward promotion. Nothing else may be scored without registration in this doc first; the selftest asserts the register stays at three cells.

1. mom12 <= 0 at entry (design 6/7 events; the momentum cell).
2. reversal_down state at entry (design 9/12; the state cell).
3. priced-calm residual-fear half: VXN > 16.47 at entry (design 16/20; the covariate cell). The cutpoint 16.47 is the frozen pre-2021 entry median: forward classification compares the entry VXN against this frozen number, so the cell boundary is deterministic and cannot drift.

Promotion rule, identical for all three: a cell is examined only when it holds n >= 10 forward rows; it is promoted only if its forward event-share has a CP95 lower bound exceeding the UNCONDITIONAL forward event share over the same rows. Below that, cells are counted silently. The rule is stated now because the priced-calm half (0.476 vs 0.800 at design, bands overlapping, era-confounded) is the most seductive object the framework has produced; its temptation is disarmed by schedule, not by willpower.

## 5. The positive proof, stated

Falsification alone is cheap; the gates above only say how the framework dies. The positive criteria, declared once:

SURVIVE every occurrence gate (compression and stress schedules, both row-counted) AND BEAT the matched climatology rows on BOTH Brier and logloss at the ftep 104-window reliability readout. Positive evidence is the only evidence: a surviving machine that loses to climatology on scoring is a curiosity, not an edge; a scoring win without gate survival is noise. Both, or nothing.

## 6. Caveats

1. The binomial gates treat forward rows as independent Bernoulli(p0) draws. Within-episode clustering (multiple rows from one spell) is absorbed by the row count, not modelled; the row-counted gate design (kill gates counted in resolved rows, not calendar time) was chosen partly for this.
2. CUM_FK's independence approximation is illustrative only (shared rows); the honest claim is "roughly one in eight, likely lower".
3. The two direction peeks are multiple looks at the same claim; both are declared in advance and no others are permitted, which is the only available mitigation.
4. The calibration expectations assume the only alternatives are the design 0.634 and the climatology 0.441; a forward world at some third value will beat both forecasts' expectations and must be read as such (a reliability diagram question, not a gate).
5. The stress gates' weaker power is a property of the design's smaller edge over a coin, not a tuning choice; no asymmetric thresholds were introduced to compensate.

## 7. Honest computation log

The first implementation of the power computation in smh_theorem.py used 1 - CDF (the survival under the alternative) instead of CDF (the kill probability under the alternative); the selftest's frozen-constant comparison caught the inversion immediately (recomputed 0.7145/0.5535/0.2757/0.0954 could not match the frozen 0.2855/0.4465/0.7243/0.9046) and the build failed until corrected. The direction of "power" - probability the gate fires when the hypothesis is FALSE - is exactly the kind of sign a pre-declared assertion exists to catch. No other corrections.

## 8. The readout operating characteristics (added same day, session 46, PROGRESS block 54)

Section 2 declared WHAT the 52/104-window reliability readouts should show on expectation. This section pre-declares what they can actually SHOW: their sampling behaviour, measured before the first window exists. One structural fact does most of the work: for two FIXED forecasts scored on the same resolved windows, the mean-score comparison reduces to a THRESHOLD ON THE EVENT COUNT K. The Brier threshold is exactly the midpoint, K > n(p+q)/2; the logloss threshold is K > n*ln((1-q)/(1-p)) / (ln((1-q)/(1-p)) - ln(q/p)). "The SMH beats the rival" means K lands on the design's side of that threshold, and nothing else. When the design forecast sits BELOW the rival's (the stress 4-week window), the win side flips.

The headline claim (compression 12 weeks, SMH 0.634 vs the matched climatology 0.441), logloss:

| resolved windows n | SMH wins at | power (design true) | false-pass (climatology true) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 26 | K >= 15 | 0.7920 | 0.1157 |
| 52 | K >= 29 | 0.8997 | 0.0605 |
| 104 | K >= 57 | 0.9714 | 0.0182 |

Brier (the looser rule where the thresholds differ by one): K >= 14/28/56, power 0.8866/0.9406/0.9821, false-pass 0.2103/0.1014/0.0289.

THE POSITIVE PROOF OF SECTION 5 IS THEREFORE EXACTLY ONE STATEMENT: at least 57 of 104 resolved compression 12-week windows resolve with the event (the stricter of the two rules). Power 0.971, false-pass 0.018. The abstract "beat on Brier and logloss" is now a count.

The reading discipline this buys, stated in advance: a NEUTRAL 52-window readout is not damnation (a true machine still loses it about 10 percent of the time) and a WINNING one is not vindication (the climatology world still wins it about 6 percent of the time). The readout is a measured instrument with measured error rates, not a verdict.

The other windows and claims, logloss, at n = 26/52/104:

| claim / window | rival | SMH wins at | power | false-pass |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| compression 4w (0.268) | climatology 0.168 | K >= 6/12/23 | 0.7350/0.7740/0.8845 | 0.2640/0.1525/0.0966 |
| stress 12w de-stress (0.636) | coin 0.5 | K >= 15/30/60 | 0.7982/0.8483/0.9111 | 0.2786/0.1659/0.0705 |
| stress 4w de-stress (0.273) | coin 0.5 | K <= 9/19/39 | 0.8545/0.9472/0.9913 | 0.0843/0.0352/0.0069 |

The 4-week compression window is the weak one: its design edge over climatology is only 0.031 nats per window (expected logloss 0.5813 vs 0.6127; if climatology is right the SMH loses 0.0281), so even at 104 windows its power is 0.884 with a 9.7 percent false-pass - recorded, never to be read as strong evidence in either direction. The stress 12-week claim sits in between (edge 0.0375 nats, 0.6557 vs the coin's 0.6931; if the coin is right the SMH loses 0.0384). The stress 4-week window wins on the OTHER side: the design says quick de-stress is unlikely (0.273 vs the coin's 0.5), so the SMH wins when FEW windows de-stress quickly - the same machinery, flipped, and frozen with its direction guard asserted by the selftest.

TIMING HONESTY: at the design compression rate (2.04 triggers per year), 26/52/104 resolved 12-week windows arrive in roughly 13/25/51 years (halved if the readout counts each row's two windows, 4-week and 12-week, separately). The occurrence gates (roughly 5/10/20/29 years at 10/20/40/60 rows) run FIRST; the readouts confirm later. The kill lines remain the binding public test; the readout schedule exists so the intermediate readouts are read correctly rather than over-read.

Caveats for this section: the thresholds assume the paired structure of the protocol's matched climatology rows (the rival forecast scored on the SAME windows, hence the same outcomes); the binomial treats windows as independent (episode dependence inherited as everywhere); the exact event-count reduction holds only for fixed forecasts, which the frozen protocol guarantees by never revising probabilities.

Honest computation log, session 46: the first scratch pass applied the "K greater than threshold" rule blindly to all four claims; on the stress 4-week window, where the design forecast (0.273) sits BELOW the rival (0.5), that produced a nonsensical 5 percent "power" - caught by sanity-checking the sign of the expected edge before anything was frozen, and fixed by carrying the win side's direction through the arithmetic. The first selftest direction guard compared the threshold against one half and failed on the compression 4-week window (both forecasts sit below 0.5 there); replaced by the correct guard (the threshold must lie between the design rate and the rival rate), which is what the selftest now asserts. No other corrections.

# SMH Definition-Elasticity Audit v0: how much of the machine belongs to the phenomenon, and how much to the definition

Status: DESIGN BOOKKEEPING on the UNSEALED pre-2021 sample only (rule-set-V class). Date filter date < 2021-01-01 throughout; holdout 2021+ SPENT and never touched. Built 12 Nov 2026, session 47, PROGRESS block 55, on Andrea's "continue developing salient hypothesis and theoretical framework"; EXTENDED the same day, session 48, PROGRESS block 56, on "continue working", with the stress mirror claim's own 9-cell grid (section 5); EXTENDED the same day, session 58, PROGRESS block 66, with the lone-print yardstick grid (section 8), computed by the TS mirror on a no-Python sandbox; its pandas counterpart's first run followed the same day, session 59, PROGRESS block 67, catching a load-order defect before freeze and then matching the mirror exactly; EXTENDED the same day, session 60, PROGRESS block 68, with the observation-horizon grid (section 9), TS mirror executed with the H=12 anchor exact; the horizon grid's first pandas run executed the same day, session 61, PROGRESS block 69, under the pre-declared fix-before-freeze: it failed exactly as pre-declared (every H > 12 cell returned the H=12 KM flat-extended, because horizon_cell reused smh_forecast's 12-frozen km_from_person_weeks), was fixed by authoring the horizon-general twin km_from_person_weeks_h inside smh_robust.py with no constant or frozen value touched, and after the fix all 5 cells matched the TS mirror exactly; the session-60 readings stand as frozen, now on executed-and-asserted numbers (both pending-first-run notes in this doc, the lone-print and horizon grids, are hereby CLOSED; chain 24 checks ALL PASS); EXTENDED the same day, session 62, PROGRESS block 70, with the climatology-counterfactual dependence audit (section 10), TS mirror executed with the C0 anchor exact and the pandas counterpart authored pending its first run inside the same smh_robust check. Nothing here is an inferential claim, a test, or a trade; the forward protocol is unchanged and the frozen definition is unchanged BY FIAT. All constants below are recomputed and asserted by the smh_robust.py selftest (verify_all's 21st check; the bundle moves from 20 to 21 checks, and the stress grid lives INSIDE that same check, no new stage; the loneprint, horizon and climato grids also live inside it).

The question this layer answers: the theorem card's headline (compression-entry cum12 0.634 vs the matched climatology 0.441) rests on three definitional choices - an 8-week rolling std for sigma_w, a 2.5-sigma event bar, and the frozen cutpoint LOW = 0.01739. If the headline were an artifact of those choices, the falsification schedule would discover it only through decades of forward rows. The audit measures the claim's DEFINITION ELASTICITY now, over a small pre-committed grid, one knob at a time, ALL cells reported, none selected. The discipline that keeps this honest: the grid was committed before any cell was computed; every cell is printed in the instrument's status card; nothing outside the grid was computed; the frozen definition does not move regardless of what the grid shows.

## 1. The grid

One knob at a time around the frozen definition (window 8, bar 2.5, cut LOW):

- THE SIGMA WINDOW: 6, 10 and 12 weeks (frozen 8), computed twice - once with the absolute cut held at LOW, once with the cut re-matched to the frozen percentile rank (27.52nd) of each window's own sigma_w distribution, so both the "same number" and the "same region semantics" readings are visible.
- THE EVENT BAR: 2.0 and 3.0 sigma (frozen 2.5). The bar scales the event rate everywhere, so both the entry clock and the climatology move together.
- THE COMPRESSION CUT: the 22.5th and 32.5th percentile of the 8-week sigma_w distribution (the frozen cut sits at the 27.52nd), the cut re-quantiled with window and bar held.

Every cell recomputes the full machinery from the canonical pre-2021 data: compression entries (cross semantics), the entry KM cum12 with its Greenwood band, and the matched climatology KM (the same clock started from every decision week) with its band.

## 2. The full table (all 11 cells)

"gap" = entry cum12 minus climatology cum12: the measured edge of the entry condition over doing nothing, under that cell's definitions. "disjoint" = the entry band lies fully above the climatology band.

## 3. The three readings

1. THE GAP SIGN IS ROBUST. The entry-minus-climatology gap is POSITIVE in all 11 cells: 0.106 to 0.222 around the frozen 0.193. Every reasonable perturbation of the definition leaves the compression cross beating the do-nothing counterfactual. The robust object in the framework is the GAP, not the level.
2. THE FROZEN DEFINITION IS NOT THE GRID OPTIMUM. The largest gap in the grid (0.222) belongs to the 12-week window at the absolute cut, not to the frozen cell, and the frozen gap sits mid-grid, not on a ridge. This is the anti-tuning evidence: the definition was frozen long before this grid was computed (the LOW cutpoint is a legacy number from the AVFI tercile tables), and the grid confirms no hand sits on the scale. The selftest carries this as a standing anchor: if recomputation ever shows the frozen cell AT the grid maximum, the anti-tuning claim needs revisiting.
3. THE DISJOINT-BAND STRENGTH IS DEFINITION-LOCAL. The entry band clears the climatology band in only 4 of the 11 cells (the frozen one included). The "bands do not overlap" sentence in the theorem card is therefore a property of the frozen definition, not of the phenomenon, and must never be quoted without the definition attached. What generalizes across the grid is the positive gap with overlapping-to-disjoint bands; what does not generalize is the strong separation.

## 4. Bookkeeping notes and structure seen in the grid

THE "TERCILE" LABEL IS HISTORICAL: LOW = 0.01739 sits at the 27.52nd percentile of the pre-2021 8-week sigma_w distribution, not the 33.3rd, which is exactly why the measured region occupancies tile 27.5/30.6/41.9 percent rather than a third each. This changes nothing - the cut is frozen as a NUMBER, not as a live quantile, and the forward protocol uses the number - but the audit states it so the label never misleads.

THE WINDOW DIMENSION MOVES THE CLIMATOLOGY MOST: the unconditional 12-week event rate swings from 0.377 (10/12-week windows) to 0.536 (6-week window) - the do-nothing baseline is the most definition-sensitive quantity in the machine, which is one more reason the forward protocol scores matched climatology rows alongside rather than trusting any fixed "unconditional" number.

THE BAR DIMENSION SCALES BOTH CLOCKS TOGETHER: cum12 moves 0.415 to 0.854 across the grid while the gap stays 0.166 to 0.193 - the event bar sets the level of both machines, and the edge between them is far less bar-sensitive than the level. The LEVEL belongs to the definition; the GAP is the phenomenon.

## 5. The stress mirror claim (added session 48): its own 9-cell grid

The compression claim is not the only forward-scored object in the theorem card. The stress de-stress claim (33 entries, 21 de-stresses, cum12 0.636 [0.472, 0.801] vs the coin 0.5) is equally forward-scored, so it gets the same audit, with one structural difference declared up front: the de-stress event is a CROSSING (next-week sigma_w <= HIGH), not a bar event, so there is NO BAR KNOB in its grid. The knobs are the window (6/10/12 weeks around the frozen 8) and the cut (absolute-cut and rank-matched conventions, plus cut-ranks 0.53/0.63 around the frozen HIGH rank). The rival is the coin 0.5, exactly as in the stress gates. The frozen HIGH rank, measured on the pre-2021 8-week sigma_w distribution, is 58.10 percent.

The full table, all 9 cells:

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| baseline (w8, HIGH) | 33 | 21 | 0.636 | [0.472, 0.800] | 0.136 | no |
| abs-cut w6 | 45 | 35 | 0.778 | [0.656, 0.899] | 0.278 | YES |
| abs-cut w10 | 26 | 16 | 0.615 | [0.428, 0.802] | 0.115 | no |
| abs-cut w12 | 27 | 20 | 0.741 | [0.575, 0.906] | 0.241 | YES |

| rank-matched w10 | 27 | 17 | 0.630 | [0.448, 0.812] | 0.130 | no |
| rank-matched w12 | 27 | 20 | 0.741 | [0.575, 0.906] | 0.241 | YES |

The three readings, mirrored:

1. THE EDGE SIGN IS ROBUST. cum12 exceeds the coin in all 9 cells: edges 0.115 to 0.283 around the frozen 0.136. De-stress within a quarter of a stress entry beats a coin under every perturbation in the grid. As with the compression claim, the robust object is the edge, not the level.
2. THE FROZEN DEFINITION IS NOT THE GRID OPTIMUM. The frozen edge sits in the LOWER THIRD of the grid; the largest (0.283) belongs to the 6-week rank-matched window. The frozen cell is CONSERVATIVE for the stress claim, and carries the same anti-tuning reading as the compression grid: no hand on the scale, and the selftest holds the same standing anchor (if recomputation ever shows the frozen cell AT the grid maximum, the anti-tuning claim needs revisiting).
3. THE BAND-CLEARS-COIN STRENGTH IS DEFINITION-LOCAL. The band's lower bound exceeds 0.5 in only 5 of 9 cells, and NOT in the frozen one ([0.472, 0.800]). This is consistent with the proof criteria's honest accounting, where the stress gates were measured weaker than the compression gates (power 17-65 percent vs coin across the schedule). The sentence "the stress band clears the coin" must never be quoted without the definition attached; what generalizes is the positive edge.

STRUCTURE: the de-stress cum12 is far LESS definition-sensitive than the event clocks (0.615-0.783 across the grid vs 0.415-0.854 for the compression claim). A crossing has no bar to scale it, so the level cannot be pumped by redefining the event; this is a structural property of crossing-type claims, not a virtue of this particular cut.

## 6. Caveats

1. n = 30 to 55 entries per cell: small, Greenwood bands wide, episode dependence inherited everywhere.
2. The grid is one-knob-at-a-time: joint perturbations (window AND bar together) were not computed, by pre-commitment, to keep the audit small and reported in full.
3. The audit is post-freeze by construction: it cannot certify the definition as "chosen without search" (nobody can), only present the evidence that the frozen cell is not a grid optimum.
4. Descriptive only: no cell feeds the forward protocol, the kill lines, the probabilities, or any instrument; no inferential language attaches to any gap or band in this document.
5. Stress grid specifics (session 48): n = 26 to 46 entries per cell, same small-sample and episode-dependence inheritance as the compression grid; the rank-matched and cut-rank cells necessarily perturb the entry SET, not just the threshold, so the cells are not a clean one-knob-at-a-time perturbation in the way the compression window cells are; the coin rival is a fixed 0.5 while the compression grid's rival (climatology) moves with the window, so the two grids' edge columns are not directly comparable.

## 7. Honest computation log

The first scratch pass of the cut-perturbation assumed the frozen cut was the 33.3rd percentile and perturbed around it (25th/40th); checking the assumption before freezing showed LOW actually sits at the 27.52nd percentile, so the committed grid was re-anchored to perturb around the true rank (22.5/32.5) before any cell value was recorded. The first version of the audit's direction guard in the selftest compared the frozen cut's percentile rank loosely; the final version asserts the rank reproduces the LOW number to 1e-4. No other corrections. The session-48 stress grid ran clean in scratch: all 9 cells computed once from the committed grid, the baseline anchor (33/21/0.636) reproduced the frozen STRESS_CUM_12W constants on the first pass, and no cell was recomputed or corrected.

## 8. The lone-print claim (added session 58): its own 5-cell yardstick grid

Status line note: this section was computed by the TypeScript mirror (research/smh_loneprint_ts_mirror.ts + research/smh_loneprint_report.json) on this no-Python sandbox, per the programme's standing two-layer convention; the pandas counterpart (the loneprint section of smh_robust.py, authored the same session) executes on the next Python-enabled reset. The C0 anchor reproduced the frozen aftermath constants EXACTLY first pass (47/46/18.5/4/43/39; windows 26, further 22/3/1, within-4 = 1), so the mirror is validated against the frozen bookkeeping.

The question this section answers: aftermath v0 section 2 froze a flagged caveat - the significance bar is ENDOGENOUS (2.5 x own sig_w), so "the expansion is one print, not many" might be an artifact of the print raising its own future bar, and "an absolute-volatility yardstick would likely show the aftermath as an elevated-variance regime". The clock claims (sessions 47/48) were audited; the lone-print claim is the remaining forward-doctrine-relevant measured claim with a flagged, unmeasured definition dependency. This grid measures it.

The pre-committed grid (one knob: the event-yardstick TYPE; bar 2.5 everywhere; the 8-week sig_w, the LOW cut and the 41-entry compression set FROZEN in every cell; all cells reported, none selected): C0 the frozen endogenous bar (the anchor); C0D the same bar restricted to the 52-week-valid domain (a domain control for C2); C1 a pooled static exogenous constant (bar = 2.5 x sigma_pooled, sigma_pooled = 0.032554, bar = 0.081386 weekly); C1D the pooled bar on the 52-week domain; C2 a slow trailing bar (2.5 x the 52-week rolling std at the decision week, mostly exogenous - the print carries only 1/52 of the weight vs 1/8 in the frozen bar).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

The four readings:

1. THE LONE-PRINT SHAPE IS MOSTLY ROBUST. The singleton share stays >= 77.8 percent in all 5 cells (frozen 90.7 percent). Under every yardstick, the modal episode is a single print: the flagged caveat's direction is confirmed but its magnitude is bounded - even against a fully exogenous bar, significant weeks are mostly isolated.
2. THE CLUSTERING INTENSITY IS DEFINITION-MADE. Median inter-arrival gap 18.5 weeks (frozen) vs 8 (slow trailing) vs 3 (pooled static); near-adjacent gaps (<=2) 4 of 46 vs 6 of 30 vs 11 of 28. Against a fixed bar the drama clusters into high-vol eras exactly as aftermath section 2 flagged: the quiet after the print is quiet AGAINST ITS OWN RAISED BAR, and the aftermath as "elevated-variance regime" is the correct reading under an absolute yardstick.
3. THE COMPRESSION-TRIGGER COUPLING IS SCALE-RELATIVE. Windows with a first event: 26 of 41 endogenous vs 12 of 41 slow-trailing vs 1 of 41 pooled-static. Compression dwells in quiet eras where an absolute bar is far away; the trigger-to-event link is a statement about the market's OWN recent scale. This is the event-side echo of the sizing study's sigma-relative/sigma-level split (lesson 15), now measured on the entry side: the clock times scale-free events; an absolute yardstick severs the trigger from the event almost completely.
4. DOCTRINE ATTACHMENT. "The first print is usually the only print" may be quoted only with the endogenous definition attached, exactly like the "bands do not overlap" sentence (reading 3 of the clock grid). The robust object is the MODALITY of lone episodes, not the isolation strength.

Caveats, frozen for this section: 6. n is small everywhere (16 to 47 significant weeks; 1 to 26 windows with events - the pooled-bar window cells are near-vacuous and are reported, not read); 7. the yardstick cells change the significant-week POPULATION, not just a threshold, so cells are not pure threshold perturbations; 8. the pooled static bar (8.14 percent weekly) is itself one arbitrary exogenous choice among many - it is reported as one cell of a pre-committed grid, never selected as "the" exogenous yardstick; 9. descriptive only: no cell feeds the forward protocol, the kill lines, the probabilities, or any instrument; the frozen definition is unchanged BY FIAT.

Computation log for this section: the anchor reproduced the frozen aftermath constants first pass; all 5 cells were computed once from the pre-committed grid; nothing outside the grid was computed; no corrections were needed.

## 9. The observation-horizon grid (added session 60): pre-commitment BEFORE any cell is computed

The three existing grids perturbed the sigma window, the event bar, the region cut, and the event yardstick - but every one of them held the OBSERVATION HORIZON fixed at 12 weeks, and the frozen 12-week horizon is itself a definitional choice that the whole forward protocol inherits: the forecast row resolves at 12 weeks, the occurrence claim is "within 12 weeks" (cum12 0.634), the capture ladder is anchored at cum4/cum6/cum12, and the climatology rival is the same 12-week clock. This grid measures the horizon elasticity of the headline before the forward protocol spends decades on it.

The pre-committed grid (one knob: the observation horizon H; the 8-week sig_w, the 2.5-sigma bar, the LOW cut 0.01739 and the cross-semantics entry set FROZEN in every cell; all cells reported, none selected):

- H = 8 and H = 10 (below the frozen 12), H = 16 and H = 20 (above it); the frozen H = 12 is the ANCHOR cell and must reproduce the frozen constants (41 entries, 327 person-weeks, cum12 0.634 [0.487, 0.782], climatology 0.441 [0.411, 0.471], median lag 5) exactly before any other cell is read.

Each cell recomputes the full machinery at that horizon: the entry KM cum(H) with its Greenwood band, the matched climatology KM cum(H) with its band (the same clock started from every sig_w-valid decision week, same horizon), the gap and the disjoint-band flag, the count of first events within H, and the median first-event lag among events within H. Readings to be frozen after computation: (a) the gap sign across H; (b) whether the frozen H=12 sits at the grid optimum (the standing anti-tuning anchor convention); (c) the disjoint-band strength as a function of H; (d) any doctrine attachment required on the "within 12 weeks" sentence.

Pre-declared expectation, stated BEFORE the run: the levels rise mechanically with H for both clocks (KM cum is nondecreasing in H), so the level comparison carries no information; the open questions are whether the GAP and the disjointness survive past 12 weeks and whether events keep accruing to entries faster than to climatology. Censoring note frozen up front: longer horizons censor late-sample entries harder (the t0+h <= len-2 rule), and the KM handles censoring by construction; the climatology pool is ~25x larger so its censoring is negligible.

Computation convention: TS mirror first on this no-Python sandbox (research/smh_horizon_ts_mirror.ts, the real canonical pre-2021 closes embedded verbatim, per the session-58 precedent), pandas counterpart authored into smh_robust.py with fix-before-freeze pre-declared, first Python execution on the next Python-enabled reset inside the existing smh_robust verify_all check. Results appended as section 9.1 below after the anchor validated.

The four readings, frozen:

2. THE FROZEN HORIZON IS THE GRID GAP MAXIMUM - HONESTLY FLAGGED, NOT HIDDEN. Unlike the window/bar/cut grids, where the frozen cell sat mid-grid or in the lower third, the frozen H=12 carries the LARGEST gap of this grid (0.193 vs 0.173 at 16 and 0.125 at 20). The anti-tuning reading therefore does NOT rest on mid-grid placement here; it rests on the recorded history: the 12-week window was frozen by pre-registration (the clock-study/ECS design) long before this grid was committed, and no horizon search ever preceded the freeze. The standing selftest anchor is inverted for this grid on purpose: it asserts the frozen-gap-at-maximum state as the RECORDED FACT (if recomputation ever shows it moving, the record needs re-reading, not the definition).
3. THE GAP DECAYS PAST 12: SALIENCE IS EDGE-LOCAL IN TIME AS WELL AS IN REGION. The gap falls monotonically beyond the frozen horizon (0.193 at 12, 0.173 at 16, 0.125 at 20), while the levels of both clocks rise mechanically. The compression cross's excess hazard is FRONT-LOADED: stretch the observation window and the entry condition erodes toward any-week climatology (by 20 weeks the bands overlap again). This is the horizon-side mirror of the capture ladder (0.634 at the cross, 0.500 after 4 quiet weeks, 0.348 after 6) and of the mid-clock's region-locality finding: the salient market condition is edge-local in region (mid entry ~ climatology) and now measured as edge-local in time. The 12-week protocol window is not arbitrary bookkeeping - it is the measured location of the edge.
4. THE DISJOINT-BAND STRENGTH IS AGAIN DEFINITION-LOCAL, AND THE NO-EVENT CLASS SHRINKS SLOWLY. Disjointness holds only at H=12 and H=16 (2 of 5 cells); at H=8/10/20 the bands overlap. The residual no-first-event class falls from 15 of 41 entries at 12 weeks to 9 of 41 at 20 (11 entries gain their first event between weeks 13 and 20; median lag rises 5 to 6), so the frozen 12-week window leaves roughly a fifth of triggers permanently unresolved in-sample - a censoring fact the forward protocol already handles by resolving rows at 12 weeks regardless.

Caveats, frozen for this section: 10. n = 41 entries in every cell, Greenwood bands wide, episode dependence inherited; 11. the horizon cells share the same entry set (unlike the cut-rank cells of section 1), so this grid IS a clean one-knob perturbation, but longer horizons censor late-sample entries harder (the KM handles it by construction; the climatology pool is ~25x larger); 12. the gap-maximum reading (reading 2) is exactly the kind of cell a tuning narrative would select - it is reported as the pre-committed analysis of a pre-committed grid, with the historical pre-registration as the only anti-tuning evidence, and no claim beyond "the frozen window was not chosen by this grid"; 13. descriptive only: no cell feeds the forward protocol, the kill lines, the probabilities, or any instrument; the frozen 12-week definition is unchanged BY FIAT.

Computation log for this section: the anchor reproduced the frozen constants on the first pass (all 10 anchor checks true); all 5 cells were computed once from the pre-committed grid; nothing outside the grid was computed; no corrections were needed on the TS side. The pre-declared expectation ("the open questions are whether the gap and the disjointness survive past 12") was answered in the negative for strength and the positive for sign: the gap survives everywhere, its strength decays.

## 10. The climatology-counterfactual dependence audit (added session 62): pre-commitment BEFORE any cell is computed

The five existing grids (sections 1, 5, 8, 9) audited the CLAIM's own definitions - sigma window, event bar, region cut, event yardstick, observation horizon. The one flagged statistical dependency NO grid has measured is the RIVAL's own band construction: the climatology counterfactual cum12 0.4409 carries a Greenwood band [0.411, 0.471] computed over the ~1050-week sig_w-valid pool in which 12-week windows OVERLAP almost completely; Greenwood's independence assumption treats those overlapping windows as independent episodes, an under-dispersed construction that FLATTERS the headline "bands do not overlap" sentence. This was flagged in the session-53/59 variable map ("overlapping-episode climatology band too narrow flagged as favouring the claim") and never measured. This grid measures it. The audit does NOT touch the frozen climatology point estimate 0.4409 (BY FIAT, it is the registered rival's forecast value in the forward protocol), and it does NOT touch the entry side (whose own episode-dependence caveat is frozen in smh_hazard_v0.md beside the claim).

The pre-committed grid (one knob: the climatology counterfactual's window-sampling / variance construction; the 8-week sig_w, the 2.5-sigma bar, the LOW cut 0.01739, the 41-entry set, the 12-week horizon and the entry band FROZEN in every cell; all cells reported, none selected):

- C0 ANCHOR: the frozen overlapping pool itself (KM cum12 + Greenwood independence band) - must reproduce 1050 windows, cum12 0.4409, band [0.411, 0.471], the 41-entry side, entry cum12 0.634 with lower bound 0.487, and the frozen disjointness TRUE, before any other cell is read.
- C1a: NON-OVERLAPPING 12-week tiles, offset 0 from the first sig_w-valid decision week (every 12th valid week as a window start), KM cum12 + Greenwood.
- C1b: non-overlapping tiles, offset 6 (the one alternative phase; both phases reported).
- C2: ERA-BLOCK DISPERSION, the dependence-honest variance estimate: the sig_w-valid pool split into 10 non-overlapping calendar blocks of 105 consecutive valid weeks (~2 years each); the climatology cum12 computed within each block; the ACROSS-BLOCK dispersion is the dependence-relevant uncertainty of the do-nothing rate (era-to-era variation). Report: all 10 block estimates, min/max, across-block sd, sd/sqrt(10), the implied LEVEL band mean +/- 1.96 x sd and the MEAN band mean +/- 1.96 x se, the share of eras whose do-nothing cum12 already sits at or above the frozen entry lower bound 0.487, and the implied effective sample size n_eff = p(1-p)/sd^2 vs the nominal 1050.
- C3: CIRCULAR-SHIFT SEAM DIAGNOSTIC, honestly expected to FAIL as a variance tool and pre-declared as such: 200 persistence-preserving rotations of the weekly return series (mulberry32 seed 42, shifts in [26, T-26]), the pooled climatology cum12 recomputed per rotation. Pre-declared expectation: the statistic is a near-invariant functional of the path (rotation only moves the censoring seam), so the surrogate sd will be near zero - the diagnostic DEMONSTRATES why the Theiler family cannot size this particular uncertainty (the pool statistic is not alignment-sensitive), which is the recorded reason C2's block dispersion is the chosen dependence measure. A seam-invariant near-degenerate distribution is the expected result, not a defect.

Readings to be frozen after computation: (a) how much wider the dependence-honest bands are than the frozen independence band (the inflation factor on the SE); (b) whether the "bands do not overlap" sentence survives the C2 level band (era dispersion) - pre-declared honest prior: it likely does NOT, and the sentence then carries BOTH its definition attachment (section 1: 4 of 11 cells) AND its dependence attachment (this grid), while the GAP (point estimates) and the forward PAIRED scoring (the 57-of-104 proof compares matched rows, never bands) are the robust objects; (c) the n_eff implied by era dispersion vs the nominal 1050 - the dependence discount on the rival's apparent sample size; (d) the doctrine attachment required on every future quotation of the disjoint-band sentence.

Computation convention: TS mirror first on this no-Python sandbox (research/smh_climato_ts_mirror.ts, the same canonical pre-2021 closes embedded verbatim, the session-60 precedent), pandas counterpart authored into smh_robust.py (the same mulberry32 LCG reimplemented bit-exactly with 32-bit masking so C3 matches across languages), fix-before-freeze pre-declared, first Python execution inside the existing smh_robust verify_all check on the next Python-enabled reset. Results appended as section 10.1 below after the anchor validates.

The ten era-block estimates, all reported: 0.438, 0.286, 0.400, 0.495, 0.381, 0.505, 0.638, 0.390, 0.410, 0.467 (min 0.286, max 0.638).

The four readings, frozen:

1. THE RIVAL'S INDEPENDENCE BAND UNDERSTATES ITS OWN UNCERTAINTY 3.4x TO 6.1x. The SE on the climatology cum12 inflates from the frozen Greenwood 0.0154 (independence) to 0.053 under non-overlapping tiles (both phases agree: 0.0531/0.0533) to 0.094 across two-year eras. The variable map's flagged risk is confirmed and quantified.

The era dispersion implies an effective sample of ~28 quasi-independent eras (p(1-p)/sd^2) versus the nominal 1050 overlapping windows - a 37x dependence discount on the rival's apparent precision, the pooled-statistic analogue of lesson 11. And three of the ten two-year eras (0.495, 0.505, 0.638) already sit at or above the entry claim's lower bound 0.487: there exist eras in which doing nothing at all carries the same 12-week significant-event probability the compression cross claims as its conservative bound. Era heterogeneity in the do-nothing rate is real, measured, and previously invisible under the independence band.

The GAP (point estimates, positive in every cell of every grid), the kill lines, the calibration expectations and the forward PAIRED scoring are untouched: the 57-of-104 positive proof compares MATCHED ROWS under never-revised forecasts, never bands, and is immune to the rival-band artifact by construction. C3's near-degenerate rotation distribution (sd 0.00537, exactly as pre-declared) demonstrates WHY: the pooled statistic is a near-invariant functional of the path, so the Theiler rotation family cannot size it - the era-block dispersion is the measured dependence variance, recorded as the standing null-tool limit for this class of pooled statistics.

Caveats, frozen for this section: 14. the frozen rival POINT estimate 0.4409 is unchanged BY FIAT (it is the registered climatology forecast in the forward protocol; the audit measures its band, never revises its value); 15. the entry side's own episode dependence remains the caveat frozen in smh_hazard_v0.md beside the claim - this grid deliberately audits only the rival, and a symmetric entry-side dependence audit would need era-block structure on n=41 entries (too few per era to estimate; recorded as unmeasurable at this n, not as a null); 16. the era blocks are calendar eras, not iid draws - the sd is era-to-era variation in the do-nothing rate, the right scale for "how uncertain is 0.441 as a level", not a sampling SE of the mean; 17. the block estimates inherit the sigma-relative definition (quiet eras mechanically produce more 2.5-sigma_w events - the 0.638 era is read only through that lens); 18. descriptive only: no cell feeds the forward protocol, the kill lines, the probabilities, the register, or any instrument; the frozen definition unchanged BY FIAT.

# SMH Clock-Keyed Sizing Study v0: does exposure sized by the clock lower max drawdown at comparable return?

Status: OPENED by Andrea's explicit authorization ("Yes, open it this session"), 12 Nov 2026, session 52, PROGRESS block 60. This reverses the session-48 standing decision "no further theory builds" for exactly this one study and nothing else. DESIGN BOOKKEEPING on the UNSEALED pre-2021 sample only (rule-set-V class). Date filter date < 2021-01-01 throughout; holdout 2021+ SPENT and never touched. Nothing here is an inferential claim, a test, or a trade, and NOTHING FEEDS ANY RULE: T, V, V4h and the forward protocol are untouched; this is a measurement of an exposure policy, not an amendment. All values below are recomputed and asserted by the smh_sizing.py selftest (verify_all's 22nd check; the bundle moves from 21 to 22 checks).

The question: the SMH measured an event hazard after compression crosses (cum12 0.634 vs climatology 0.441) and a post-print suppression. Does an exposure policy that reduces size in the armed windows (and, in the vol-targeted arms, scales size to realized vol) lower MAX DRAWDOWN at comparable return versus its unscaled counterpart, on the pre-2021 unsealed sample? The honest prior, declared in advance: sizing shapes losses, not returns; return is expected to fall roughly with mean exposure, and the drawdown benefit is uncertain because the armed hazard is direction-agnostic (big weeks either way) while the 2008-type crash mass lived in the stress region, which the vol-target arms handle mechanically as vol rises. If the armed windows do not overlap the drawdown mass, the armed component will do little and the study will say so.

## 1. The design (committed before any cell was computed)

No lookahead: every exposure decision for week t uses only data through the close of week t-1. All six cells are evaluated on the IDENTICAL common window: all weekly closes t with date < 2021-01-01 where sig_w(t-1), mom12(t-1) and the simple return r(t) are all defined (at least 13 prior weeks of data). Weekly portfolio return = m(t) * r(t), cash earns zero, no costs (the stage-5 finding: costs do not move the needle at this holding grain). Equity compounds weekly; drawdown is peak-to-trough on the weekly-close equity.

Frozen conventions reused from the SMH instruments, not reinvented: sig_w = 8-week rolling std (ddof=1) of weekly log returns including the current week; LOW = 0.01739, HIGH = 0.02697; a compression entry at week t' is sig_w(t') <= LOW with sig_w(t'-1) > LOW (the frozen 41-entry semantics). ARMED(t) = week t falls in the 12 weeks after any compression entry t' <= t-1 (a new cross re-arms; overlapping windows do not stack; the armed clock runs the full 12 weeks with no early termination at an event - the frozen claim window is 12 weeks, and cutting it short would be a second, untested policy).

The six pre-committed cells, ALL reported, none selected:

| cell | m(t) |
|---|---|
| C0 buyhold | 1 always |
| C1 armed_half | 0.5 if ARMED(t) else 1 |
| C2 voltarget | min(1, 0.15 / vol8ann(t-1)), vol8ann = sig_w(t-1) * sqrt(52) (the registered V convention) |
| C3 vt_armed | C2(t) * (0.5 if ARMED(t) else 1) |
| C4 tmom | 1 if mom12(t-1) > 0 else 0 (the T convention) |
| C5 tmom_armed | C4(t) * (0.5 if ARMED(t) else 1) |

## 2. The pre-declared margins (the design claims, falsifiable)

Three paired comparisons, each judged only against its own unscaled arm:

- C1 vs C0, C3 vs C2, C5 vs C4.
- PASS requires BOTH: max drawdown of the sized arm <= 0.75 times the unscaled arm's (a 25 percent relative reduction, chosen because the one prior sizing change in programme history cut drawdown by a third in-sample), AND total return of the sized arm >= 0.70 times the unscaled arm's total.
- Anything else is a FAIL and is recorded dead in this document and the session log. No re-tuning this session: the grid is the grid, no cell outside it is computed.
- Calmar (CAGR / |maxDD|) is reported as a secondary metric with NO decision attached.

## 3. Mechanism diagnostics (reported alongside, no decision attached)

- Armed occupancy of the common evaluation window.
- The share of the 10 worst buy-hold weeks that fall in armed windows (with the occupancy baseline stated, so the lift is visible).
- The C0 max-drawdown episode's date range and its armed share.

## 4. Results (computed once, from the committed design, session 52)

Common evaluation window: 2000-12-22 through 2020-12-25, 1045 weeks, identical for all six cells (41 compression entries pre-2021). Armed occupancy of the window: 0.4105.

| cell | total return | CAGR | max DD | Calmar | mean exposure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C0 buyhold | +3.918 | +0.0828 | -0.6948 | 0.119 | 1.000 |
| C1 armed_half | +2.156 | +0.0591 | -0.6948 | 0.085 | 0.795 |
| C2 voltarget | +4.592 | +0.0897 | -0.3845 | 0.233 | 0.794 |
| C3 vt_armed | +2.672 | +0.0671 | -0.3845 | 0.175 | 0.603 |
| C4 tmom | +2.992 | +0.0716 | -0.2493 | 0.287 | 0.666 |
| C5 tmom_armed | +1.961 | +0.0557 | -0.2493 | 0.223 | 0.502 |

The pair verdicts, per the pre-declared margins (magnitude comparison on drawdown):

- C1 vs C0: max DD 0.6948 vs 0.6948 (need <= 0.75x: NO); return 2.156 vs 3.918 (need >= 0.70x: NO). FAIL.
- C3 vs C2: max DD 0.3845 vs 0.3845 (NO); return 2.672 vs 4.592 (NO). FAIL.
- C5 vs C4: max DD 0.2493 vs 0.2493 (NO); return 1.961 vs 2.992 (NO). FAIL.

ALL THREE PAIRS FAIL, and the failure is diagnostic, not marginal: the armed halving changed max drawdown by EXACTLY ZERO in every pair (identical to four decimals) while cutting total return 30 to 42 percent. The mechanism diagnostics say why:

- ZERO of the ten worst buy-hold weeks (2001-09-21 at -17.4 percent, 2008-10-10 at -13.2 percent, through 2001-03-02 at -8.8 percent) fell in armed windows, against 41.05 percent armed occupancy. The lift is exactly zero.
- The deep-drawdown weeks (C0 drawdown <= -10 percent: 598 weeks) are UNDER-represented in armed windows: 34.45 percent against 41.05 percent occupancy.
- Every max-drawdown episode (C0-C3: 2001-01-19 to 2002-10-04, 90 weeks; C4-C5: 2000-12-22 to 2002-02-08, 60 weeks) has armed share 0.0000. With no armed week inside any peak-to-trough path, the drawdown depth is mathematically untouched by the armed halving, which is why the max DD is identical rather than merely similar.

## 4a. The reading

The armed-reduction policy is DEAD, per the pre-declared margins, and the study's value is the mechanism it exposes: THE CLOCK'S EVENT IS SIGMA-RELATIVE; EQUITY DRAWDOWN MASS IS SIGMA-LEVEL. A 2.5-sigma week against a quiet compression base is a small absolute move; the 0.634 hazard arms often (41 percent of the calendar) but arms small absolute weeks. The weeks that actually destroy equity require a HIGH sigma base, which is the stress region, and by the time a crash week arrives the clock crossed UP long before. The clock times scale-free events; the drawdown is made of scale. They are different phenomena, and the armed policy was aimed at the wrong one.

The lever that DID work on this sample, descriptively, with no decision attached: the vol-target arm (C2, the registered V convention). Max drawdown -0.3845 versus -0.6948 for buy-hold (a 45 percent reduction) with total return HIGHER (+4.59 versus +3.92) at mean exposure 0.79, Calmar nearly doubled (0.233 versus 0.119). Vol targeting shrinks exposure exactly where sigma is high, which is where the drawdown mass lives; it needs no clock at all. The momentum arm (C4, the live T convention) has the best Calmar of all six (0.287). Both observations are in-sample for their conventions, carry no decision, and promote nothing: the study's pre-declared claims were about the armed component, which failed.

## 5. Caveats

1. Descriptive design bookkeeping on one unsealed sample: episode dependence everywhere (the 2000-2002 and 2008 drawdowns dominate), no inferential language attaches to any cell.
2. The vol-target cells inherit the registered V convention (15 percent annualized target, capped at 1) unchanged; the cap is a real design choice and its effect is inseparable from the arm.
3. The armed policy is deliberately coarse (a fixed half-size, a fixed 12-week window): a finer policy (event-terminated arming, graded cuts) would be a new study requiring new pre-registration, not a re-tune of this one.
4. Nothing here touches T, V, V4h, the kill lines, the probabilities, or the forward protocol.
5. The C2 observation (vol targeting cutting drawdown while raising return) is in-sample for the 15 percent target convention, which was registered from the same era; it is reported because it was a pre-committed cell, not because it earns anything. The study's decision-attached claims were only the three armed pairs.
6. The armed policy's failure is specific to the pre-committed coarse form (fixed half-size, fixed 12-week window, no early termination). The mechanism finding (armed windows contain no drawdown mass) makes any finer armed policy strictly unpromising on this sample, but the formal dead verdict attaches to the pre-registered form only.

## 6. Honest computation log

Two corrections were caught before anything was frozen. First, the scratch pair-verdict compared the negative max-drawdown values directly ("maxdd_sized <= 0.75 * maxdd_unscaled"), an inequality that trivially evaluates true when the drawdown is unchanged; caught while reading the scratch output, corrected to the magnitude comparison before any verdict was recorded. The verdicts are FAIL under both forms (the return criterion fails independently), but only the corrected form is honest about WHY the drawdown criterion fails: the depth did not move at all. Second, the precision-reprint script had a transcription bug (equity = cumprod(m*r) instead of cumprod(1+m*r)), which produced garbage numbers and briefly looked like a data problem; the canonical data was checked (1058 pre-2021 rows, zero bad closes) and found clean, the bug was found in the reprint script, and the original computation stood unchanged. The instrument's selftest initially asserted too strong a no-lookahead property (that an entry week is never armed); an entry week can legitimately be armed by a previous entry's still-active window, so the assertion was replaced with an independent brute-force recomputation of the armed flag (armed(t) = some entry t' <= t-1 with 1 <= t - t' <= 12), which the implementation matches exactly. No cell value changed in any of these corrections.

# SMH AVFI sp_z Covariate Study v0: does the spread z-score at the compression entry add hazard information beyond the clock?

Status: OPENED by Andrea's explicit authorization ("Run it this session"), 12 Nov 2026, session 54, PROGRESS block 62, under the same protocol as the sizing study: the design below was committed BEFORE any cell was computed; the cells were then computed once, all reported, none selected; the verdict follows the pre-declared rule; no re-tuning. DESIGN BOOKKEEPING on the UNSEALED pre-2021 sample only (rule-set-V class). Date filter date < 2021-01-01 throughout; holdout 2021+ SPENT and never touched. Nothing feeds T, V, V4h, the kill lines, the probabilities, or the forward protocol; the seductive-cell register stays CLOSED at three cells. All values are recomputed and asserted by the smh_spz.py selftest (verify_all's 23rd check; the bundle moves from 22 to 23 checks).

The question (Andrea's Layer 3, the one untested cell): does AVFI sp_z at a compression entry - the spread z-score, the indicator's own mean-reversion measure, present in the frozen series but never once examined in any SMH document - add predictive information beyond the existing clock? The honest prior, declared in advance: null expected. Every neighbouring covariate verdict is null (the transitions orthogonality, the Cox duration-only verdict, the stress and mid clocks' nothing-conditions-them findings), the one prior sp_z test in programme history was the entry-timing filter that produced zero trades ever, and the decision bar below is deliberately set high.

## 1. The design (committed before any cell was computed)

Data: the frozen 41 pre-2021 compression entries; the person-week expansion exactly as the hazard layer built it (327 person-weeks, 26 first events, the corrected v0.1 construction); sp_z taken from the frozen avfi_series.csv at the entry week (the series as it stands - no new feature construction of any kind).

Missing-data rule, committed now: entries where sp_z is undefined at the entry week are excluded from every test, counted, and reported; all comparisons run on the same non-NaN subset so they stay paired.

The three pre-committed tests, ALL reported:

- T1, THE EXTREME SPLIT (descriptive KM): entries with |sp_z(entry)| >= 1.0 versus < 1.0 - the threshold pre-exists from the H-number era and is not tuned here. Report n, first events within 12 weeks, and the Kaplan-Meier cum12 with Greenwood 95 percent bands per half.
- T2, THE SIGNED EXTREMES (descriptive counts only, no verdict): within the extreme subset, the sp_z >= +1 and sp_z <= -1 cells reported as counts; a half with fewer than 5 entries is reported as a count only and draws no sentence.
- T3, THE COX PROTOCOL (the decision test): discrete-time cloglog hazard on the person-weeks, leave-one-episode-out cross-validated Brier over the folds, the exact v0.1 construction. The pipeline is validated FIRST by reproducing the frozen duration-only LOYO Brier 0.1596 on the full 41-entry set to tolerance; then duration-only versus duration + sp_z on the non-NaN subset, both out of fold.

## 2. The pre-declared verdict rule

sp_z is recorded as ADDING INFORMATION beyond the clock ONLY IF the duration + sp_z leave-one-out Brier improves on duration-only by at least 2.0 percent relative (Brier <= 0.98 times duration-only) on the paired subset. The 2.0 percent bar sits deliberately ABOVE the 1.4 percent out-of-fold improvement the entire three-covariate block (momentum sign, AVFI state, compression depth) managed in v0.1: a single late-tested covariate must beat the whole block to earn anything. Any other outcome is recorded as ADDS NOTHING.

Divergences: declared OUT OF SCOPE, honestly. No frozen divergence definition exists anywhere in the programme, and constructing one now would be a post-hoc feature with free parameters - exactly the forking the discipline refuses. If a divergence definition is ever frozen in advance, it becomes a new pre-registered study, not an extension of this one.

Seductive discipline: if T1 or T2 shows a large split, it is flagged seductive and untrusted (small n, selected look, the era-confound lesson), and it is NOT registered as a forward-scored cell; the register is closed at three, and registering a fourth requires explicit authorization.

## 3. Results (added after computation, session 54)

### 3.0 The anchor investigation (the pre-registered validation step, and what it found)

The pre-registered T3 required reproducing the frozen session-32 Cox v0.1 LOYO Brier pair (duration-only 0.1596, full 0.1574) before any sp_z comparison. The reproduction FAILED: under the exact v0.1 construction (person-week cloglog, leave-one-episode-out, pooled per-row Brier over 327 rows) the duration-only LOYO Brier computes 0.07406, and no construction consistent with the frozen coefficients can produce 0.1596: with the frozen duration slope +0.0129 the out-of-fold predictions are near-constant at the pooled rate 26/327 = 0.0795, which forces a per-row Brier near 0.073-0.075 (a near-constant prediction of about 0.37 would be needed for 0.1596), and an episode-level Brier at base 26/41 cannot mathematically fall below 0.232. Investigation across the bundle: the pair exists ONLY in session-32 prose (session log, PROGRESS, the Cox doc, KNOWLEDGE); the frozen session-34 TypeScript replication contains NO Brier at all, and its independent LR columns (duration vs constant LR 0.05) already contradicted a duration-slope Brier story. Conclusion, recorded as a correction: the session-32 LOYO Brier pair was frozen from scratch prose with no instrument and is a defective artifact, the same failure mode the standing person-week-reconstruction policy was adopted to prevent. It is corrected here, on the record.

The pipeline was then validated against the frozen session-34 TS replication instead (research/smh_hazard_cox_v01_report.json), which reproduces EXACTLY: intercept-only beta -2.4907, -loglik -90.7663; duration-only beta [-2.5625, +0.0129], -loglik -90.7413; and the session-32 full-model coefficients [-3.081, +0.017, +0.225, +0.502, -1.596] reproduce to three decimals. The corrected LOYO Briers (pooled per-row, 41 folds, all frozen constructions otherwise): intercept-only 0.07364, duration-only 0.07406, full four-covariate 0.07607. The corrected reading is HARSHER than the defective one: the covariates make out-of-fold prediction WORSE by 2.71 percent (the defective pair claimed a 1.4 percent improvement), and duration-only does not beat intercept-only either (+0.6 percent worse), consistent with the session-34 LR reading that the weekly hazard is indistinguishable from constant at n = 41. The frozen v0.1 verdict - the hazard content is DURATION (here read honestly as: the clock's pooled level, not even its slope), not the entry conditions - survives and strengthens. docs/smh_cox_v01.md is corrected accordingly (its section 4 now carries these numbers with the defective pair's fate documented).

### 3.1 The coverage finding (the study's real headline)

sp_z exists in the frozen avfi_series.csv only from 2015-04-10 onward (the AVFI construction's own warm-up; 606 of 1365 rows defined). Of the 41 pre-2021 compression entries, 10 carry sp_z at entry and 31 are NaN, excluded per the pre-registered rule. The last untested Layer-3 cell is therefore closed primarily by data coverage: the covariate does not exist for three quarters of the design sample, and no amount of marginal comparison recovers that. Entry-week sp_z on the defined subset: min -1.372, median -0.081, max +2.388.

### 3.2 T1, the extreme split (descriptive KM)

| half | n | first events | cum12 | 95 percent band |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sp_z >= 1 in magnitude | 6 | 2 | 0.333 | [-0.044, 0.711] |
| sp_z < 1 in magnitude | 4 | 2 | 0.500 | [0.010, 0.990] |

Both bands span most of the unit interval (the lower edge of the first is trivially zero). No readable difference; nothing seductive; nothing registered. The full-sample anchor for comparison: 41 entries, 26 events, cum12 0.634 [0.487, 0.782].

### 3.3 T2, the signed extremes (counts only)

sp_z >= +1: 3 entries, 0 first events. sp_z <= -1: 3 entries, 2 first events. Both halves sit below the pre-declared 5-entry sentence threshold: counts only, no sentence, per the committed rule.

### 3.4 T3, the decision test (cloglog LOYO Brier, non-NaN subset)

Subset: 10 episodes, 104 person-weeks, 4 first events. Duration-only LOYO Brier 0.03860; duration + sp_z 0.03790. Relative improvement 1.80 percent, BELOW the pre-declared 2.0 percent bar (which itself sat above the entire three-covariate block's defective-claimed 1.4 percent). VERDICT, per the pre-declared rule: sp_z ADDS NOTHING. With 4 events the comparison is decorative anyway; the coverage finding and the corrected null carry the closure.

## 4. Caveats

1. Descriptive design bookkeeping on one unsealed sample; no inferential language attaches to any cell.
2. n = 41 entries split into halves; the bands are wide and episode dependence is inherited everywhere. The T1 halves are 6 and 4 entries; the T2 halves are 3 and 3; the T3 subset is 10 episodes with 4 events. Nothing here could have cleared any evidential bar even before the verdict rule applied.
3. The |sp_z| >= 1 threshold is inherited, not selected, but it is still a single point on a continuous covariate; the split table is descriptive, and the decision test is T3.
4. The 2015-04-10 coverage boundary is itself an era effect: every defined entry falls in the post-2015 design sub-era, so T1/T2/T3 are all conditional on that era in addition to being conditional on compression. This is reported, not corrected for; correcting for it is impossible at this n.
5. The corrected LOYO values in section 3.0 use the pooled per-row construction, the only construction consistent with the frozen coefficients; the defective pair's construction is unrecoverable (no instrument, no code, no report JSON ever existed for it).
6. Nothing here touches T, V, V4h, the kill lines, the probabilities, the register, or the forward protocol.

## 5. Honest computation log

1. First scratch dispatch used a hard-coded /home/user/projects/quant data path for the AVFI series; the path was wrong for the frozen loader (smh_forecast reads the bundle's own AVFI constant) and the environment reset had wiped the projects tree. Fixed by importing AVFI from smh_forecast; no data issue.
2. The pre-registered anchor (0.1596) failed on the first clean run (computed 0.07406). Per the pre-registration, computation STOPPED and the discrepancy was investigated before anything was recorded: the session-32 pair proved unreproducible and inconsistent with the frozen coefficients; the session-34 TS replication (no Brier in it) provided the exact validation anchors instead. The correction is frozen in the smh_spz.py selftest, which asserts BOTH the exact reproduction of the TS anchors and the NON-reproduction of the defective pair.
3. One intermediate syntax error in a scratch heredoc (a malformed conditional expression) and one numpy shape typo (np.ones(n, 1) instead of np.ones((n, 1))); both caught by the interpreter before any number was produced; no result was ever computed from buggy code.
4. All frozen values in the instrument were computed once from the committed design, at full float precision, and are asserted by the selftest at tolerance 0.0005 (values 0.0015 or wider would also pass; the embedded values are exact recomputations).
5. verify_all ran BEFORE the freeze (22 checks ALL PASS) and after the smh_spz stage was added (23 checks ALL PASS). Nothing was booked; the forward log stays empty by design; the Sunday 2026-11-15 cadence is unchanged.
